2026-07-30 — feat/arm-pi-connection — arm control page: controls-first layout, merged session bar, persisted connection log
================================================================================
Follow-up to the arm bring-up earlier today. With the arm actually usable, the control
page's layout became the bottleneck — it had grown by accretion during ARM-01 and buried
the three controls an operator actually uses.

LAYOUT. Teleop / Record / Replay moved to the top; RUN TASK sits below them. Arm connection
became a sticky right-hand side panel at lg+ so health stays visible while working, with a
one-line compact variant under the title on narrow screens. Both variants are driven by the
same derived state inside RobotArmConnectionPanel (a new `variant` prop) rather than
duplicated markup, so they cannot drift apart. Root width went max-w-5xl -> max-w-7xl to fit
the second column, and the page's own p-4 was dropped since cart-mfg/+layout.svelte already
applies p-4 lg:p-6 (it was doubled).

MERGED SESSION UI. The page carried TWO active-session displays that could contradict each
other: RobotArmControlBar, and an inline banner at +page.svelte:140-172 whose stop form had
no confirm and no use:enhance. The banner is deleted. RobotArmControlBar absorbed its states
and is now the single indicator, sitting directly above the controls it governs.

The subtle part: `active` (live from the Pi) and `holder` (derived from Mongo
robot_arm_runs via deriveArmHolder) are different sources that CAN disagree — a run whose
terminal webhook never landed leaves a holder row set forever while the Pi reports nothing
running. That is exactly the 70-day STALE row seen this morning. Precedence is now explicit:
the Pi is authoritative for WHETHER something is running, Mongo for WHO and SINCE WHEN. The
orphaned case renders STALE and states out loud that it blocks Run Task, so a ghost row
explains itself rather than being a mystery.

Two long-standing bugs in that component, both fixed:
  - elapsed() had no hour/day rollover, so a 70-day run rendered "101939m 3s". Now "70d 8h".
  - currentUserId was accepted as a prop but never used — because the component's LOCAL
    Holder interface omitted userId (ArmHolder in robot-arm-lock.ts:33 has it), which made
    the comparison un-writable against the declared type. The prop was not vestigial, the
    type was incomplete. Wired up per ARM-01 §8.4: Stop no longer prompts you to confirm
    stopping your own run, only someone else's.

CONNECTION LOG. Reuses the existing immutable DeviceEvent collection (30-day TTL) — no new
model, no schema change. Recorded from the BIMS side rather than pushed by the Pi, because
the event that matters most — "BIMS cannot reach the arm" — is only observable from here.

Writes on state change, plus an hourly floor while steady. The heartbeat is deliberately
coarse: /devices/+page.server.ts:19 renders DeviceEvent.find() UNFILTERED with limit(100),
so a 60s arm heartbeat (~1440 rows/day) would have buried an unrelated page. A 30s dedupe on
changes absorbs Refresh-button mashing and instantaneous flapping.

device_events carries applyImmutableMiddleware, which blocks updateOne/findOneAndUpdate — so
no atomic upsert throttle is available and the read-then-write is unavoidably racy across
concurrent tabs. Accepted deliberately: worst case is two adjacent same-state rows a few ms
apart in an append-only TTL'd telemetry log that nothing reads for correctness.

"Last connected" folds in the observation from the current request rather than reading the
newest success row. Reading the row directly would report an hour-old timestamp next to a
green ONLINE dot, since a steady arm only writes hourly. When down, it falls back to a wider
query rather than claiming the arm has never connected.

RUN TASK GATE. Kept exactly as-is — it is the UI half of a server-side re-check in startTask
(+page.server.ts:174-184) that is the real enforcement, and DRY_RUN=false currently blocks
it by design (ARM-01 §7.4). Only the message improved: it now names the reason and the exact
fix (set DRY_RUN=true in ~/robot-arm/.env, restart robot-arm), and the already-running case
points at the session bar.

HOUSEKEEPING. safeActive() normalized to {active, activeError} matching safePreflight(),
which deleted the hand-written isActive/isError union guards the page carried.

KNOWN TRADEOFF: RobotArmControlBar keeps sticky bottom-0 for phone thumb-reach, but sticky
only pins while the element's own flow position is on screen — moving it down to sit with
the controls means it pins across less of the scroll range than before. Judged worth it
since it now sits directly above the Start buttons, but it is a partial regression against
ARM-01 §8.4's "always one thumb-reach away".

NOT DONE, flagged for a separate commit: all four robot-arm AuditLog writes
(control/+page.server.ts:208,242 and calibrate/+page.server.ts:57,83) use field names that
do not exist in the AuditLog schema — resourceType, resourceId, userId, username, timestamp,
details. Mongoose strict mode silently drops them, so those audit rows land with NO
attribution at all, just _id/action/changedAt. Real bug, ~4-line fix, but not a UI change.

Also noted but not acted on: device_events has exactly one index (the TTL on createdAt), so
the connection-log read filters on an unindexed deviceId and reverse-scans the TTL index.
Bounded by the 30-day TTL and fine at current volume; if device_events grows past ~100k docs
add { deviceId: 1, createdAt: -1 }. That is a shared-model change affecting every
device_events writer, so it wants its own review.

`npm run check`: 40 errors / 434 warnings — IDENTICAL to the pre-change baseline (40), and
zero errors and zero warnings in the four touched files.

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-feat-ar-2c5b1f-brevitest.vercel.app
  (immutable: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-duzxteid7-brevitest.vercel.app)
Source: branch feat/arm-pi-connection @ commit 33a0603e
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout feat/arm-pi-connection at commit 33a0603e
  (or: git log feat/arm-pi-connection --oneline to find it if the branch has moved on)
What this deployment is for: the reorganized arm control page — controls first, connection

2026-07-30 — feat/box-inventory-disconnect — disconnect Box as inventory source
================================================================================
Box no longer supplies parts inventory. PartDefinition becomes BIMS-owned master
data, edited directly rather than wiped and re-imported on every sync.

Root cause this addresses: syncPartsFromBox() ran deleteMany({}) on
part_definitions and then re-upserted every row from the Box spreadsheet "Live
Equipment Overview.xlsx", so any manual correction was destroyed on the next
sync. The sync had also been failing since 2026-03-04 (lastSyncStatus "partial",
E11000 dup key on partNumber: null) and its Box access token expired 2026-03-20,
so the part rows were already effectively BIMS-owned in practice.

2026-07-30 — master — Claude-driven SPU reassembly inventory adjustment (MCP tools + agent API)
================================================================================
New capability: an operator can tell Claude (via the BIMS MCP connector) "Reassembly completed
on SPU 203 with all magnets in heating block used" and Claude resolves the parts, presents a
per-SPU confirmation list (SPU barcode + last-5 header, one row per part with fill-in-the-blank
quantities — per Alejandro's Figure 1 mock), and only after explicit user approval deducts
inventory. Same part on multiple SPUs = separate quantity per SPU. Vague prompts with no
identifiable parts → Claude asks the user to scan the SPU + part barcodes.

Built:
- src/routes/api/agent/inventory/parts/+server.ts (NEW): GET part resolver — scanned barcode
  (exact, falls back to partNumber), exact partNumber, or free-text q where every token must
  match name/description/partNumber/category ("screw upper metal bracket" → the bracket screw).
  Optional bomType=spu|cartridge filter. Returns candidates w/ live inventoryCount.
- src/routes/api/agent/inventory/reassembly/+server.ts (NEW): POST atomic per-SPU deduction.
  Resolves SPUs by _id/udi/barcode with unique-suffix fallback ("203", "71dbc"); parts by
  barcode/partNumber/exact name. Validates EVERYTHING first — any unresolved ref or non-positive
  quantity rejects the whole request with per-entry errors, nothing deducted. Requires
  confirmed:true (agent's assertion the human approved the list). Applies via the existing
  recordTransaction service (type 'consumption' → decrements PartDefinition.inventoryCount,
  fires low-stock notification) + one AuditLog per SPU. Response returns previous→new counts.
- InventoryTransaction model + recordTransaction service: added optional spuId field + index so
  reassembly deductions are queryable per SPU (additive schema change).
- src/routes/api/agent/inventory/spu-assembly-map/+server.ts (NEW): GET the active SPU work
  instruction as a step→parts map (title/text/partNumber/qty per step). This is how Claude
  resolves assembly-CONTEXT language part names can't ("the screw for the upper metal bracket"
  matches no part name — it's WI knowledge). Steps live in versions[].steps.partRequirements
  (partName is stashed in .notes), current version = wi.currentVersion. NOTE: today's active WI
  is sparse (v1, 1 step, 10 parts) — the map gets useful once Alejandro uploads the detailed
  SPU build instruction set he offered.
- src/lib/server/mcp/bims-mcp.ts: three new tools — parts_lookup, spu_assembly_parts_map, and
  record_reassembly_parts_usage. The record tool's description encodes the mandatory workflow
  (resolve vague parts → clarify ambiguity → no parts named = ask for barcode scans → show the
  Figure-1-style per-SPU quantity list → only call with confirmed:true after user approval).

TESTED live against Atlas on a local dev server (port 5199, env from the second working copy's
.env): parts_lookup "magnet" → the 3 magnet parts + upper bracket (PT-SPU-008/009/010/012 with
live counts); partNumber + barcode paths OK. Reassembly gates: confirmed:false → 400; unknown
part/SPU → per-entry errors, nothing applied; qty 0 → rejected; suffix "0202" resolved
BT-M01-0000-0202. Full end-to-end: deducted 1x PT-SPU-009 on SPU ...0202 (875→874, tx
PxiLXoMfIPSzQ-aGN5cBp, spuId-linked, AuditLog written) then REVERSED with a documented
adjustment tx (874→875) via raw driver. MCP layer verified: tools/list = 34 (31+3), tools/call
parts_lookup("main magnet spherical") → PT-SPU-008 over the wire.

Also: this working copy was missing @modelcontextprotocol/server + zod in node_modules (deps
were in package.json; nobody had npm-installed since the MCP work landed) — `npm install` fixed
it, which is also why /api/mcp 500'd locally before. npm run check DROPPED 41 → 10 errors (all
10 pre-existing, none in the new files; the old 31+ were all cannot-find-module fallout).
package-lock.json diff is just npm normalizing stale "peer" flags. Not committed/deployed yet.

Component map added same session (WIMF-SPU-01 v18 docx from Alejandro): curated component→parts
map in src/lib/server/services/spu-component-parts.ts (12 components: base structure, drive,
LRA, heater block, timing belt/stage, stage board, structure top, upper magnet bracket,
scanner/main board, antennas, enclosure, labels) served by spu-assembly-map with live
inventory cross-check. Cross-check found: PT-SPU-104 (40 Tooth GT-2 Pulley — the WI v18 part;
old PT-SPU-017 is 20-tooth) NOT in part_definitions; label parts PT-SPU-056/057/058/059/070/072
not in inventory; antennas PT-SPU-101/102/103 exist but inactive at 0; PT-SPU-099 Aluminum Tape
count 0. Alejandro says two new parts are coming — tracked in the map, NOT added to inventory.

2026-07-31 — master — Atlas query-targeting alerts: root causes + index fixes
================================================================================
Atlas fired "Query Targeting: Scanned/Returned > 1000" on all 3 nodes starting 17:08Z.
Full repo audit + live Atlas index/size snapshot + Query Insights (Jacob) findings:

CONFIRMED OFFENDERS (fixed by adding indexes / narrowing filters):
1. ot2_bridge_commands sweep-status lookup (kind + payload.sweepRunId) — polled in a loop
   by DeckLoadingGrid during every deck scan; NO index → full 50K-doc scan per tick.
   Query Insights #1: 14.5K exec/day, 19.2 min total, 35,997 examined:returned.
   → index {kind, payload.sweepRunId}.
2. kanban replenish page AuditLog.find({recordId: /^replenishment:/}) without tableName —
   full 35K-doc audit_log scan per view (52,116 ratio in QI) → filter now includes
   tableName so the {tableName,recordId} compound serves it.
3. /api/kanban/wip-mtime watermark poll: findOne().sort({updatedAt:-1}) on kanban_tasks,
   polled every few seconds per open board, no updatedAt index → index {updatedAt:-1}
   (+ {status,archived,statusChangedAt} for the archive cron).
4. Robot-arm control page DeviceEvent reads (deviceId+eventType) — only a createdAt TTL
   index existed → index {deviceId,eventType,createdAt:-1} (also covers equipment detail
   + ask-bims device reads).
5. opentrons health-poller singleton: OpentronsRunRecord.find({status:$in}) every 15s
   forever with no status-prefix index → index {status}.
6. validation_sessions had ZERO indexes (magnetometer poll + history) → {spuId,startedAt},
   {type,startedAt}.
7. agent_messages had ZERO indexes (3 endpoints filter by user/status) → {toUserId,createdAt},
   {fromUserId,createdAt}, {status,createdAt}.
8. cartridge_records (14.5K docs @ 35KB!): dashboard sort({updatedAt:-1}), createdAt-window
   counts, and $exists aggregates (QI ratio 164,818 on one shape) → {updatedAt},{createdAt},
   {reagentInspection.status},{waxQc.status}.
Indexes build automatically on deploy (Mongoose autoIndex).

ARCHITECTURE FLAGS (decisions needed, not yet changed):
- scanner_events: 1,575,522 docs, no TTL — heartbeat telemetry accumulating forever.
  Recommend TTL (e.g. 14d heartbeats / 90d scans) or move heartbeats out of Mongo.
- ot2_bridge_commands: 50K docs @ 9.1KB avg (~460MB) — completedAt TTL exists (7d) but
  pending/failed/claimed docs never expire; payloads are fat. Recommend TTL on createdAt
  or slimming payloads.
- cartridge_records avg 35.4KB with embedded photos[] + 30+ indexes — doc-size and
  index-count pressure on the biggest collection; longer-term: move photos to cv_images
  refs only.
- traceability/bom search: unanchored case-insensitive regexes (user-triggered, lower heat).
- Secondaries also alerted → some reads use secondaryPreferred (likely the research app
  sharing the cluster) — same fixes apply cluster-wide once indexes build.
npm run check: 11 errors — unchanged baseline.

Second follow-up ("someone needs to add an inventory mutation to the MCP server" on claude.ai):
diagnosed via this session's own BIMS connector — the connector CONNECTS fine to live prod
(inventory_overview returns current Atlas counts) but claude.ai still serves the pre-feature
31-tool list: record_reassembly_parts_usage / parts_lookup / spu_assembly_parts_map are absent
from its cached tools/list even though the server (verified sha 8d86d539) registers 34. Ruled
out wrong-host: the alejandro-valdezs-projects Vercel twin is behind SSO (a connector there
could not connect at all), so the connector must point at the live main domain. Root cause is
claude.ai's per-connector tool-list cache. Server-side mitigation: bumped McpServer version
1.0.0 -> 2.0.0 (bump on every tool add/remove from now on). User-side fix required once:
remove + re-add (or edit/save) the BIMS connector in claude.ai Settings -> Connectors to force
a tools/list re-fetch, then start a new conversation.

================================================================================
2026-07-31 — feat/robot-arm-pose-calibration — multi-pose joint-map calibration UI
================================================================================
Built the BIMS-side UI for the multi-pose robot-arm calibration whose API landed on the
Pi earlier today (separate repo C:\Users\aleja\robot-arm, branch feat/multi-pose-calibration
@ 17144e2). Sync zero captures ONE matched pose and can therefore only ever produce an
offset; the leader and follower are geared differently (1/345 vs 1/191 vs 1/147), so a 1:1
teleop mirror over-travels on any joint whose ratios disagree — the gripper is the visible
casualty. Capturing several poses lets the Pi least-squares fit a per-joint scale as well.

Files: src/lib/server/robot-arm-client.ts (getJointMap/capturePose/deletePose/
clearJointMap against /calibrate/map*, plus JointMapRecord + JointFitStatus types),
src/routes/manufacturing/cart-mfg/robot-arm/calibrate/+page.server.ts (joint-map load +
three actions), and .../calibrate/+page.svelte (pose capture, per-pose delete, per-joint
fit table with status and live tracking error).

Two real defects were caught by a pre-push review before the push, both verified by hand
against source rather than taken on the reviewer's word:

1. Serverless timeout overrun. svelte.config.js sets maxDuration: 30. The calibrate load
   awaits getActive -> getCalibration -> getJointMap strictly sequentially (the serial bus
   is single-owner, so Promise.all is not available the way control/+page.server.ts uses
   it). Adding the third call took the worst case from 5+15=20s to 5+15+15=35s. Against an
   unreachable Pi — the normal case from Vercel, since the host lives on the tailnet —
   every timer runs to completion and Vercel 504s the invocation, which defeats the whole
   point of the safeCalibration/safeJointMap wrappers. Cut the live timeouts 15s -> 10s
   (5+10+10=25s) and made safeActive report reachability, so a host that never answered
   skips the live reads entirely instead of taking the most expensive path.

2. AuditLog writes were being silently discarded. auditLogSchema declares tableName/
   recordId/action/oldData/newData/changedAt/changedBy/... — not the resourceType/
   resourceId/userId/username/timestamp/details shape documented in CLAUDE.md. Mongoose
   strict mode (no global override in connection.ts) drops unknown paths on create, so
   every calibration mutation would have persisted as {_id, action, changedAt}: an audit
   row that looks written but records no operator and no fit data. Rewrote the three new
   actions to the real schema (rich payload into newData, which is Schema.Types.Mixed).
   NOTE: the two PRE-EXISTING actions in the same file (capture, clear, for sync zero)
   still use the non-conforming shape and are still being silently dropped — left alone as
   out of scope, but they are a live QMS gap. CLAUDE.md's "Form Actions" example documents
   the wrong field names and is what propagated this; worth correcting at the source.

Also fixed: a whitespace hole in the deletePose index guard (Number('   ') === 0 would have
silently deleted pose 0), an unguarded fit.status.filter that would 500 the entire route
during SSR on a malformed joint_map.json, and copy that asserted "No joint map saved.
Teleop mirrors the leader 1:1." when the host was merely unreachable — on a page whose job
is telling the operator whether the arm is calibrated, that is the one thing it must not
state backwards.

npm run check: 57 errors / 426 warnings on this branch; zero diagnostics reference any of
the three touched files.

NOT YET TESTABLE END-TO-END: the Pi's running process still serves pre-calibration code.
Its working tree is at 17144e2 with the endpoints present, but robot-arm.service has been
up since 2026-07-30 15:31:03 CDT — before the checkout — so GET /calibrate/map returns 404.
The unit is system-scoped, brevitest3 has no passwordless sudo and root SSH is key-denied,
so the restart needs a human: ssh brevitest3@172.16.28.191 'sudo systemctl restart robot-arm'

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-feat-ro-e765c4-brevitest.vercel.app

Retrieve this exact code: git checkout feat/robot-arm-pose-calibration at commit 76eb1841
  (or: git log feat/robot-arm-pose-calibration --oneline to find it if the branch has since moved on)

2026-07-31 — master — multi-pose calibration UI promoted to production
================================================================================
Promoted feat/robot-arm-pose-calibration to master. Rebased onto 28c5ef35b (master had
moved 14 commits); only progress.txt conflicted — both sides had appended entries, both
kept in chronological order. The code commit applied cleanly with no conflicts.

npm run check: 61 errors / 426 warnings, zero of them in the three touched files
(robot-arm-client.ts, calibrate/+page.server.ts, calibrate/+page.svelte). The count rose
from 57 purely because master's 14 commits added 18 files and 4 errors of their own.

FIELD RESULT — the feature was used against real hardware the same day and found the
gripper (servo 6) bug it was built for. Diagnosis, in order:
  - Ruled out clamping: follower gripper Min/Max_Angle_Limit are 0/4095, i.e. unset.
  - The leader gripper travels 1973..3335 (1362 ticks); the follower claw only ~354.
    The follower runs OUT OF TRAVEL at roughly 35% of the leader's squeeze, so the
    relationship SATURATES — it is not one straight line. Active-region slope ~0.72,
    flat thereafter.
  - The original fitted scale of 0.8231 therefore kept climbing past the follower's
    mechanical stop (+707 ticks at full squeeze). On an STS3215 that stalls the motor,
    trips overload protection, and CUTS TORQUE — the claw goes slack. The reported
    symptom "won't close all the way" was over-commanding, not under-travel.
  - Early pose sets made this invisible: captures clustered in the open third of the
    range (5%..36%), with duplicate poses and one mid-range outlier giving segment
    slopes of 0.335 and 1.297 either side of it.
  - After cleanup the fit is honest: gripper scale 0.7187 residual 3.8 (was 170), and
    shoulder_pan residual fell 112 -> 2.9.

OUTSTANDING, in priority order:
1. Follower servo 6 needs Max_Angle_Limit set (~2530) so the existing clamp in
   _mirror_goal stops the goal at the closed position. Until then ~796 ticks of squeeze
   past the stop remain unprotected and the stall returns on a hard grip. EEPROM write,
   reversible by writing 4095 back. Not done — needs running on the Pi directly.
2. STALE-INDEX DELETE BUG (shipped, known). Pose delete posts a positional index, but
   the Pi renumbers every pose on each mutation (_build_map enumerates and reassigns).
   Deleting one row shifts everything below it, so a second delete against the page's
   original numbering removes the WRONG pose. This bit the operator today: it destroyed
   a good open/close pose pair that had to be physically recaptured. Fix is to post
   captured_at (already rendered on every row) and resolve it server-side. Was flagged
   as a warn in the pre-push review and shipped anyway at the operator's direction.
3. wrist_roll still reads insufficient_range (span 7 ticks across all poses) with a live
   tracking error near 886. It is the joint with the cord-tangle history; deserves its
   own capture pass.
4. The two PRE-EXISTING sync-zero actions in calibrate/+page.server.ts (capture, clear)
   still write the non-conforming AuditLog shape and are still being silently dropped by
   Mongoose strict mode. The three new joint-map actions were fixed to the real schema.
   CLAUDE.md's Form Actions example documents the wrong field names and is the source.

Deployment: production — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb.vercel.app
Source: branch master @ commit c93d3d17

  (or: git log master --oneline to find it if master has since moved on)

raw device output is explicitly requested. Prod-verified on SPU 249: criteria 3900-4500,
5 wells, all cells PASS, matching the BIMS screenshot exactly.
